Under subject-disjoint evaluation, PhysioAI achieves $99.03\pm1.34\%$ on KiMoRe Overall, $94.64\pm7.36\%$ on the Hard-67 stress test, and $87.44\pm7.69\%$ on UI-PRMD Overall. These results exceed the strongest comparator for each endpoint by $0.27$, $2.87$, and $1.33$ percentage points (pp), respectively. These findings demonstrate that structured clinical knowledge can serve as an effective source of training-time supervision for physiotherapy action recognition.

</details>


### [25] [RoES: Rotational Equivariant Selective-frequency Fusion for Multimodal Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12497)
*Jiabao Wang,Wenjian Liu,Yaoming Cai,Gengyu Zhang,Boyan Zhao,Zijia Zhang,Yao Ding,Xiaobo Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RoES: a rotation-equivariant selective-frequency fusion network for infrared-visible image fusion that decouples low- and high-frequency components with a trainable rotation-enhanced updater/predictor, using a rotation-equivariant Mamba for low-frequency and a polar spectral attention-based Dual-Fourier block for high-frequency refinement, achieving state-of-the-art fusion quality and downstream object detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Infrared-visible fusion is ill-posed; uniform rotation equivariance across all features can degrade high-frequency, modality-specific details. A selective-frequency approach that enforces equivariance on low-frequency structures while preserving flexibility for high-frequency details can improve both fusion quality and downstream tasks.

Method: Introduce a trainable rotation-enhanced updater/predictor to dynamically decouple low- and high-frequency components. Low-frequency structures are modeled with a rotation-equivariant Mamba to capture long-range dependencies. High-frequency details are refined by a polar spectral attention-based Dual-Fourier block guided by explicit low-frequency information. The network uses a dual-branch fusion module for spectral consistency and is optimized end-to-end.

Result: Extensive experiments show RoES achieves state-of-the-art performance in both fusion quality and downstream object detection, demonstrating effectiveness of frequency-selective, equivariant fusion. The paper also provides source code at the given GitHub link.

Conclusion: Selective-frequency, rotation-equivariant fusion effectively reconciles shared and modality-specific information in infrared-visible fusion, offering a robust solution for multimodal fusion and promising improvements for related downstream analyses.

Abstract: Infrared-visible image fusion facilitates robust multimodal perception by integrating complementary textural nuances from visible sensors with thermal signatures from infrared systems. Due to the task's inherently ill-posed nature, existing methods heavily rely on structural priors but typically enforce rotation equivariance uniformly across all features. Such a holistic approach overlooks a critical distinction where low-frequency shared structures strictly adhere to equivariant constraints while high-frequency modality-specific details require greater flexibility to preserve unique information. To bridge this gap, we propose RoES, a Rotational Equivariant Selective-frequency fusion network. Instead of employing static decomposition, we introduce a trainable rotation-enhanced updater/predictor module to dynamically decouple low- and high-frequency components. The resulting representations are then processed through a dual-branch fusion module tailored for spectral consistency. Specifically, a rotation-equivariant Mamba is employed to capture long-range structural dependencies in the low-frequency domain, while a polar spectral attention-based Dual-Fourier block refines high-frequency details under explicit low-frequency guidance. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RoES consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance in both fusion quality and downstream object detection, establishing a robust solution for multimodal fusion by reconciling frequency-selective features with equivariant constraints. The source code is available at https://github.com/BryceLosky/RoES-Fusion.

</details>


### [26] [LettuceVisSim: A Simulator That Generates Lettuce Image Time-series for Vision-Based Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12505)
*Ziye Zhu,Bert van 't Ooster,Congcong Sun,Eldert van Henten,Sjoerd Boersma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LettuceVisSim is a combined lettuce growth simulator that integrates a process-based shoot dry weight model, a canopy layout algorithm, and a Unity-based renderer to generate labeled time-series crop images for vision-based reinforcement learning in controlled-environment agriculture. It is validated across multiple datasets, demonstrates fast image generation, and provides a proof-of-concept RL demonstration using solely simulated images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the scarcity of labeled crop images for vision-based RL in controlled-environment agriculture (CEA) by providing a simulator that can produce labeled, time-series image data with plant-state grounding.

Method: A PBM for shoot dry weight dynamics; a canopy layout algorithm deriving canopy representations from shoot dry weight; a Unity rendering engine producing RGB and segmentation images. Validation includes comparing PBM to dynamic plant-density data (R^2=0.84), mapping DW to potential projected area (R^2=0.94), validating canopy layouts against 12 experimental datasets (R^2=0.84 when driven by measured DW; R^2=0.40/0.76 when driven by PBM-simulated DW), and demonstrating sub-10 ms rendering; a RL demonstration trained on LettuceVisSim-generated images.

Result: Five key findings: (1) PBM reproduces shoot dry weight under dynamic density with R^2=0.84; (2) mapping DW to potential projected area yields R^2=0.94; (3) canopy layout validation across 12 datasets reproduces ground coverage ratio dynamics with R^2=0.84 (measured DW) and R^2=0.40 (0.76 w/o outlier) (PBM DW); (4) Unity renderer outputs RGB/segmentation images in under 10 ms; (5) a lighting-control policy can be learned from LettuceVisSim images, demonstrating vision-based RL feasibility in CEA.

Conclusion: LettuceVisSim provides a viable, efficient tool for generating labeled image data for vision-based RL in CEA with validated mappings and fast rendering. Its effectiveness depends on the fidelity of the PBM and the canopy model, as evidenced by performance drops when using PBM-driven inputs; future work could extend validation to more crops, diversify environmental conditions, and enhance realism and generalization of learned policies.

Abstract: Vision-based reinforcement learning holds strong potential for decision-making in controlled environment agriculture (CEA). However, its development is hindered by the scarcity of labelled crop images. To address this gap, LettuceVisSim, a lettuce growth simulator that generates labelled time series of crop images, was developed and validated. The simulator contains a process-based model (PBM) for shoot dry weight dynamics, a canopy layout algorithm for deriving canopy layout representations from shoot dry weight, and a Unity rendering engine for image generation. Five findings support the simulator. First, the PBM reproduced shoot dry weight under dynamic plant-density management with $\mathrm{R}^{2}=0.84$. Second, a piecewise cubic regression mapped shoot dry weight to potential projected area with $\mathrm{R}^{2}=0.94$. Third, the canopy layout representation was validated using 12 experimental datasets each having different dynamic environmental and spacing conditions. It reproduced the ground coverage ratio dynamics observed in measured images, achieving $\mathrm{R}^{2}=0.84$ when driven by measured shoot dry weight and $\mathrm{R}^{2}=0.40$ (0.76 excluding one outlier) when driven by PBM-simulated values. Fourth, the Unity rendering engine converted canopy layout representations into RGB and segmentation images at less than 10~ms. Fifth, a demonstration showed that a lighting-control policy can be learned and applied by observing only crop images that were generated with LettuceVisSim, providing a proof of concept of vision-based reinforcement learning in CEA using LettuceVisSim.

</details>


### [27] [ChitraMiti: Benchmarking Visual Grounding and Modality Reliance in Bengali Geometric Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12509)
*Khan Raiyan Ibne Reza,Sanjana Aktar Maria,Sumaiya Tabassum Nimi,Md Adnan Arefeen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces Bengali geometry benchmarks (ChitraMiti-12.8k; 12.8k synthetic problems) and NCTB-500; a three-phase evaluation protocol shows description-only text can substitute diagram info for VLMs, but cross-modal verification remains weak; fine-tuning helps but a gap to zero-shot models persists; dataset/code publicly available.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Evaluate and benchmark vision-language models on multimodal geometry reasoning in a low-resource language (Bengali), addressing lack of benchmarks that combine diagrams with textual descriptions and questions.

Method: Three-phase protocol: (1) diagram-only inputs, (2) diagram-plus-description, (3) description-only; evaluate five open-weight and closed-source VLMs; construct ChitraMiti-12.8k (12,874 Bengali planar geometry problems with 15-attribute descriptions) and NCTB-500 (500 diagram-extracted items); conduct supervised adaptation by fine-tuning on ChitraMiti-12.8k and testing on ChitraMiti-1k and NCTB-500.

Result: Description-only performance is statistically indistinguishable from diagram-plus-description across models, indicating structured descriptions are a sufficient proxy for evaluation; models struggle with cross-modal verification and can be misled by swapped spatial relations; fine-tuning improves performance on ChitraMiti-1k and NCTB-500 but a large gap remains to the best zero-shot model.

Conclusion: The proposed datasets and evaluation protocol enable standardized study of Bengali multimodal geometry reasoning and text-visual cross-checking; making code and data public enables reproducibility; advances in VLMs should focus on cross-modal verification and accurate alignment between text and diagrams.

Abstract: Evaluation of vision-language models (VLMs) for multimodal mathematical reasoning remains limited for low-resource languages and for geometry problems that require reading a diagram and a question together. We introduce ChitraMiti-12.8k, a synthetic benchmark of 12,874 Bengali planar geometry problems paired with structured 15-attribute descriptions, and NCTB-500, a complementary set of 500 diagrams manually extracted from Bengali school textbooks. Using a three-phase protocol that separates diagram-only, diagram-plus-description, and description-only inputs, we show across five open-weight and closed-source VLMs that description-only performance is statistically indistinguishable from diagram-plus-description performance, establishing structured descriptions as a sufficient textual proxy for controlled evaluation. Despite this, models remain poor at cross-modal verification, frequently misled by a swapped spatial relation even when they answer the unmodified item correctly. We further evaluate supervised adaptation on ChitraMiti-12.8k, finding that fine-tuning improves performance on both ChitraMiti-1k and NCTB-500, although a substantial gap to the strongest zero-shot model remains. Together, ChitraMiti-12.8k, NCTB-500, and our evaluation protocol offer a standardized way to study Bengali multimodal geometry reasoning and, more broadly, whether VLMs actually check their text against what they see. Our dataset and code are publicly available on Hugging Face at https://huggingface.co/datasets/RaiyanKhaan/ChitraMiti.

</details>


### [28] [One Skill Does Not Fit All: Automatic Discovery and Taxonomy-Guided Routing of Frame-Selection Skills for Long-Video Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12517)
*Jian Hu,Zixu Cheng,Da Li,Wei Li,Ziquan Liu,Shaogang Gong*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AutoSkill is a source-supervised framework for adaptive frame-selection in long-video QA, routing questions to executable frame-selection skills discovered iteratively from a small labelled source pool. It induces a target-like taxonomy from unlabelled data and assigns per-question skills at inference, enabling a frozen video MLLM to operate with selected frames.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frame-selection strategies for LVQA vary in effectiveness across question types and benchmarks; a fixed strategy underutilizes valuable evidence and wastes budget. Adaptive, skill-based evidence acquisition can improve performance while maintaining a limited frame budget.

Method: Initiate with a small labelled source pool. LLM agents iteratively propose, implement, evaluate, and refine candidate frame-selection skills. For a target benchmark, use only unlabelled question/option text to induce a shared semantic taxonomy and rewrite labelled source examples into the target style; estimate a category-to-skill mapping. Do not use target videos or answers in this phase. At inference, assign each question a single skill and use frames accordingly with a frozen video MLLM.

Result: On five long-video benchmark splits, AutoSkill improves Qwen2.5-VL-7B by 2.4% and Qwen3.5-4B by 1.2%, demonstrating effectiveness of adaptive skill-based frame selection for LVQA.

Conclusion: AutoSkill shows that source-supervised, iterative skill discovery and routing can adaptively select evidence for LVQA without target labels, achieving consistent gains across multiple benchmarks and model sizes.

Abstract: Long-Video Question Answering (LVQA) requires locating decisive evidence in hour-scale videos under a limited frame budget. Most training-free methods apply the same frame-selection strategy to all questions, despite substantial variation in the evidence required by different question types. Our analysis shows that the relative effectiveness of frame-selection strategies varies across semantic categories and benchmarks, motivating adaptive evidence acquisition. In this paper, we introduce AutoSkill, a source-supervised framework for automatically discovering and routing executable frame-selection skills. Starting from a small labelled source pool, LLM agents iteratively propose, implement, evaluate, and refine candidate skills. For a target benchmark, AutoSkill uses only unlabelled question and option text to induce a shared semantic taxonomy, rewrite labelled source examples into the target style, and estimate a category-to-skill mapping. Neither target videos nor target answers are used in this process. At inference time, each question is assigned one skill, which selects the frames used in a single inference of the frozen video MLLM. Across five long-video benchmark splits, AutoSkill improves Qwen2.5-VL-7B and Qwen3.5-4B by 2.4% and 1.2%, respectively, demonstrating the effectiveness of our AutoSkill.

</details>


### [29] [Aligned Radiometric RGB-Thermal Fusion for UAV Facade Anomaly Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12521)
*Yuan Yang,Shulei Li,Haobo Liang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Sensor-level RGB–thermal fusion for UAV facade inspection; introduces per-sensor correction, RGB→thermal registration, common-support cropping, and signed 16-bit radiometric encoding; releases M3T dataset; shows mixed, category-specific gains from radiometric contrast over RGB alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Geometric misalignment and weak thermal contrast between sensors hinder spatial correspondence and anomaly detection in facade inspection; a unified pipeline and dataset are needed to quantify benefits of radiometric contrast.

Method: Per-sensor correction; RGB→thermal registration; common-support cropping; signed local contrast encoding of 16-bit radiometric data; input to a compact single-stream detector; creation of M3T (674 RGB–radiometric samples across 5 projects, 8 anomaly categories); four-fold project-level evaluation; ablation studies; evaluation on RGBT-Tiny.

Result: Median registration error: 3.384 pixels. Controlled-displacement analysis characterizes local contrast response under displacement. Four-fold evaluation yields mean average precision (mAP) 0.168 across IoU 0.5–0.95 with 28.50 GFLOPs per image. Single-split ablation shows improved delamination detection with thermal/combined inputs over RGB-only and alternative thermal inputs, but aggregate accuracy does not surpass RGB alone. RGBT-Tiny shows mixed performance with rendered thermal imagery.

Conclusion: Aligned radiometric contrast offers category-specific benefits and limitations for compact facade screening; radiometric encoding can augment RGB in certain cases, but does not universally improve detection performance.

Abstract: Unmanned aerial vehicle facade inspection can combine red, green, and blue (RGB) imagery with thermal measurements to screen surface and subsurface anomalies. However, geometric discrepancies between the sensors and thermal image rendering can obscure spatial correspondence and weak temperature contrasts. This article presents a sensor-level pipeline comprising per-sensor correction, RGB-to-thermal registration, common-support cropping, and signed local contrast encoding of 16-bit radiometric measurements. The encoding preserves the distinction between locally hotter and colder regions and supplies the fourth input channel of a compact single-stream detector. We introduce M3T, a dataset of 674 paired RGB and radiometric thermal samples from five facade-inspection projects covering eight component and anomaly categories. The median residual registration error is 3.384 pixels, and a controlled-displacement analysis characterizes how the local contrast response changes under controlled displacement. Project-grouped four-fold evaluation yields mean average precision of 0.168 over intersection-over-union thresholds from 0.5 to 0.95, using 28.50 billion floating-point operations per image. A separate single-split ablation shows improved delamination detection over RGB-only and alternative thermal inputs, although aggregate accuracy does not improve over RGB alone. Evaluation on RGBT-Tiny shows mixed performance with rendered thermal imagery. These results characterize the category-specific benefits and limitations of aligned radiometric contrast for compact facade screening.

</details>


### [30] [Earth-Agent-Pro: Towards Real-World Full-Chain Earth Observation with Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12533)
*Zhutao Lv,Chenhao Dang,Yi Feng,Yanpei Gong,Xiaolei Wang,Junyan Ye,Conghui He,Weijia Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Earth-Agent-Pro: an execution-adaptive plan-and-execute framework for real-world EO agents; introduces Earth-Bench-Pro benchmark with expert task cores; shows improved LLM-as-Judge and grounding via adapters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable full open-world EO execution from high-level questions to executable workflows with runtime evidence, bridging planning, execution, and evidence repair; current setups under-test only partial chains.

Method: Plan-and-execute framework with expert-authored skills; workflow-centered memory; suffix repair; two adapters (planner and executor); Earth-Bench-Pro with 248 task cores (744 questions) across RGB, spectral, and RS; evaluation with GPT-5 backbone; joint adapter tuning for Qwen3.5-9B; planning-only and execution analyses.

Result: Earth-Agent-Pro achieves 66.13% LLM-as-Judge accuracy, +20.95 vs ReAct; +24.44 in Tools-In-Order; adapter tuning raises Qwen3.5-9B LLM-as-Judge from 38.31% to 50.00%; planning improves planning; grounding improves execution; code/datasets to be released.

Conclusion: Demonstrates effective open-world EO execution with memory-augmented planning and adapters; Earth-Bench-Pro enables large-scale evaluation; future release of code/datasets; potential impact on EO AI workflows.

Abstract: Real-world Earth observation (EO) agents must translate high-level scientific questions into executable workflows to acquire observations, prepare data, perform domain computations, and derive conclusions from runtime evidence. Existing EO agents typically start from supplied observations, while benchmarks typically provide prepared inputs or candidate answers, leaving full-chain open-world EO execution largely untested. We present Earth-Agent-Pro, an execution-adaptive Plan-and-Execute framework using expert-authored skills to constrain planning and runtime tool use. Workflow-centered structured memory records planned steps, accepted evidence, and their dependencies, enabling repair of only the affected workflow suffix when runtime evidence invalidates a step. Separate large language model adapters use sequence-level supervised fine-tuning for planner workflow composition and node-level group relative policy optimization with locally verifiable rewards for executor tool-argument grounding. Earth-Bench-Pro instantiates 248 expert-curated task cores as 744 questions under three matched regimes. Its 248 Open-World Execution questions span RGB imagery, spectral observations, and remote sensing products, pairing high-level requests with runtime data requirements, executable trajectories, and open-ended answers grounded in execution evidence. With a shared GPT-5 backbone, Earth-Agent-Pro achieves 66.13% LLM-as-Judge accuracy, exceeding ReAct by 20.95 points in this metric and 24.44 points in Tools-In-Order. Joint adapter tuning raises Qwen3.5-9B LLM-as-Judge accuracy from 38.31% to 50.00%, an 11.69-point gain over the untuned configuration. Planning-only evaluation and execution with the reference workflow show that the adapters improve workflow composition and argument grounding, respectively. Code and datasets will be released soon.

</details>


### [31] [RelateAnything: Real-Time Open-Vocabulary Relation Prediction From Any Inputs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12552)
*Maëlic Neau*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RelateAnything is a 53M-parameter open-vocabulary relation detector that accepts image regions and a text-based predicate vocabulary at inference, using a text-embedding bank instead of learned classifiers, achieving 2.3–3.5x recall over open-vocabulary baselines while being fast and transferable.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Open-vocabulary relation modeling in scene graphs is hampered by fixed vocabularies, label-conditioned architectures, and evaluation metrics biased toward training corpora; a model that can predict arbitrary predicates without retraining is needed.

Method: RelateAnything takes an image and region proposals, outputs relation scores for a user-supplied predicate vocabulary (strings). It uses no object labels as input; region sources can vary; predicate bank is a text embeddings. Training uses RA-4M: 474k images, 4.3M region-predicate relations over 10,102 free-text predicates generated against numbered box markers and geometrically verified. Positive-unlabeled supervision and a text encoder that separates antonyms (contrastive embeddings with cosine distance ~0.95). Evaluation uses OV-SGG-Bench with six axes across datasets. Runtime ~20 ms/frame.

Result: On three cross-dataset benchmarks and a zero-shot setting, RelateAnything achieves 2.3–3.5x mean recall over the strongest open-vocabulary method of comparable scale; gains persist with a real detector; outperforms a 3B-VLM scene-graph model on both metrics with under 2% of its parameters; in-domain measurements overstate transfer gains ~5x.

Conclusion: Open-vocabulary relation detection is feasible at moderate scale; the model, data, and benchmark are public, enabling reproducibility and broader evaluation; RA-4M provides large-scale free-text supervision for relation prediction.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary detection accepts any class list at inference, and promptable segmentation returns regions without class names: the taxonomy has left the model and become an input. Relation prediction has not. Scene-graph models are still trained and evaluated on the 50 or 56 predicates of one annotation style, their relation head conditioned on object labels and so tied to one detector. Three obstacles explain this, none primarily modelling: no relation corpus is both free-text and verified, a label-conditioned architecture cannot accept a vocabulary it was not trained on, and the standard metric rewards agreement with the training corpus, so a larger vocabulary scores as a regression. We present RelateAnything, a 53M-parameter model taking an image and regions from any source and returning scored relations over a predicate vocabulary supplied at inference as strings. Object labels are never an input, so the region source can change without retraining, and the vocabulary is a bank of text embeddings, not a learned classifier. It runs at 20 ms/frame. Training over 19,103 predicates requires positive-unlabeled supervision and a text encoder that separates antonyms, which contrastive encoders embed at cosine 0.95. To supply the supervision we build RA-4M, 474k images and 4.3M relations over 10,102 free-text predicates, generated against numbered box markers and geometrically verified. To measure it we build OV-SGG-Bench, six axes scored across datasets that the priors standard recall rewards cannot satisfy. On three cross-dataset benchmarks and a fourth zero-shot, RelateAnything has 2.3-3.5x the mean recall of the strongest open-vocabulary method of comparable scale, margins that survive a real detector, and leads a 3B-VLM scene-graph model on both metrics at under 2% of its parameters. In-domain measurement overstates transfer gains ~5x. Model, corpus and benchmark are public.

</details>


### [32] [DRS-VPT: Directly Relocalizing in a Scan with Vision Point Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12557)
*Lanke Frank Tarimo Fu,Maurice Fallon*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A transformer-based, end-to-end image-to-scan registration model (DRS-VPT) regresses scan pose, per-point maps, and per-camera poses/maps from images and a 3D point cloud, with a coarse-to-fine feature pyramid for direct reprojection; it unifies calibration and relocalization tasks and achieves state-of-the-art on image-to-LiDAR registration with strong zero-shot transfer.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and streamline downstream tasks in autonomous driving and indoor localization—camera-LiDAR calibration and camera-to-map relocalization—by learning a single model that aligns images to scans and predicts complete pose/map estimates across views.

Method: A feed-forward transformer that takes query images and a reference 3D point cloud, and outputs the scan pose, per-point map, and per-camera poses/maps all expressed in the first camera frame. It also predicts a coarse-to-fine pyramid of per-point and per-pixel features to enable direct reprojective alignment of the scan to the first image.

Result: Single DRS-VPT model achieves state-of-the-art performance for image-to-LiDAR registration in autonomous driving, competitive indoor relocalization without map-specific weights, and strong zero-shot transfer to unseen environments; qualitatively, the model learns complex scan-to-image projection properties such as occlusion of back-facing points.

Conclusion: The approach unifies calibration and relocalization tasks into a single framework with strong cross-domain performance and interpretable projection behavior, highlighting the potential of feed-forward transformers for joint registration tasks.

Abstract: We present DRS-VPT, a feed-forward transformer architecture for foundational image-to-scan registration. Given query images and a reference 3D point cloud, the model predicts the scan pose and point map alongside the poses and point maps of each camera, all expressed in the first camera's frame. It additionally predicts a coarse-to- fine pyramid of per-point and per-pixel features for direct reprojective alignment of the scan to the first image. This formulation unifies downstream tasks such as camera-LiDAR calibration in autonomous driving and indoor camera-to-map relocalization. A single DRS-VPT model achieves state-of-the-art performance for image-to-LiDAR registration in autonomous driving, competitive indoor relocalization without training map-specific weights, and strong zero-shot transfer to unseen environments. We also show qualitatively that the model learns complex scan-to-image projection properties such as occlusion of back-facing points.

</details>


### [33] [KAD-Net: Kinematics-Aware Decoupled Learning for Robust 3D Hand Pose Estimation from a Single Depth Image](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12559)
*Jun Lu,Zhenming Chen,Lin Chen,Kanlun Tan,Xiaoling Li,Qiao Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KAD-Net combines a kinematics-aware decoupled learning approach for robust 3D hand pose estimation from a single depth image, featuring a Finger Topology Constraint module and a task-decoupled hierarchical multitask framework to improve distal joint representation and reduce mutual interference between 2D localization and depth regression; achieves state-of-the-art results on ICVL, NYU, and MSRA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hand kinematics are highly complex and exhibit strong joint dependencies; self-occlusion and depth ambiguity limit accurate distal joint localization. Traditional multitask architectures often force a shared feature space for 2D joint localization and depth estimation, causing negative transfer and degraded performance.

Method: 1) Finger Topology Constraint (FTC) module uses three consecutive finger joints to build a local kinematic representation that imposes topological constraints and helps locate occluded distal joints by leveraging visible context. 2) A task-decoupled hierarchical multitask framework separates 2D joint localization from depth estimation, with a dedicated multitask strategy for depth regression to isolate UV (2D) and depth features and mitigate interference.

Result: Extensive experiments on benchmark datasets (ICVL, NYU, MSRA) show that KAD-Net achieves state-of-the-art accuracy in 3D hand pose estimation and outperforms existing methods.

Conclusion: KAD-Net provides improved robustness to occlusion and more accurate distal joint localization through kinematic-aware and decoupled learning. It has potential for HCI, VR, and gesture-based control applications.

Abstract: Due to the complexity of hand kinematics and self-occlusion, existing 3D hand pose estimation methods based on single depth images struggle to comprehensively model the topological dependencies among hand joints. Furthermore, traditional hierarchical multitask architectures enforce a shared feature space for both 2D joint localization and depth estimation, which can induce mutual interference. To address these challenges, we propose a Kinematics-Aware Decoupled Learning Network (KAD-Net) for robust 3D hand pose estimation. Specifically, we first design a Finger Topology Constraint (FTC) module to enhance the representation of distal joints. This module utilizes three consecutive finger joints to construct a local kinematic representation to impose topological constraints, which supplements the kinematic features of the distal joints. The FTC module leverages the structural context from visible joints to assist in locating occluded distal joints, thereby improving robustness to occlusion. Additionally, we propose a task-decoupled hierarchical multitask framework. This framework separates 2D joint localization from depth estimation and incorporates a dedicated multitask learning strategy for depth regression, effectively isolating the UV and depth features to mitigate mutual interference and negative transfer. Extensive experiments demonstrate that KAD-Net outperforms existing methods on several benchmark datasets (ICVL, NYU, and MSRA), achieving state-of-the-art accuracy in 3D hand pose estimation. Potential applications of KAD-Net include human-computer interaction, virtual reality and gesture-based control systems.

</details>


### [34] [An Ultra-Widefield Swept-Source OCTA Dataset and a Polar-Gated Mamba Network for Retinal Vessel Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12574)
*Yang Liu,Yibing Shen,Keming Zhao,Cenk Jiang,Zhenghang Qian,Zhicheng Du,Chen Xiong,Qidong Shao,Zijun Lin,Yunqi Hu,Jingjing Zhou,Lian Zhang,Peter E. Lobie,Peiwu Qin,Chengming Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces WOIVES, the first public ultra-widefield (24x20 mm^2) SS-OCTA vessel-segmentation dataset (206 eyes) with soft probability annotations, and proposes PG-Mamba, a visual state-space model with polar-coordinate scan orders and Dynamic FOV Gating for improved vessel segmentation; achieves superior cross-validated performance and lower errors on key vascular metrics; datasets and code are publicly available.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: There is a lack of public benchmarks for vessel segmentation in ultra-widefield SS-OCTA to enable quantitative vascular analysis across refractive ranges.

Method: WOIVES collects UWF SS-OCTA images with broad refractive error range and provides soft probability vessel annotations. PG-Mamba introduces a visual state-space framework that leverages two complementary polar-coordinate scan orders and an auxiliary Dynamic FOV Gating module to modulate feature representations at the bottleneck.

Result: PG-Mamba outperforms seven competitive baselines on broad segmentation metrics under cross-validation and yields the lowest median absolute errors for vessel density, fractal dimension, and vessel length density.

Conclusion: WOIVES is publicly available on Zenodo and PG-Mamba code is on GitHub, enabling public benchmarking and replication for quantitative vascular analysis in UWF SS-OCTA.

Abstract: Ultra-widefield (UWF) swept-source optical coherence tomography angiography (SS-OCTA) enables large-area retinal vascular imaging, yet vessel segmentation at this scale lacks dedicated public benchmarks and comprehensive evaluation for quantitative vascular analysis. We introduce WOIVES, to our knowledge the first publicly available UWF SS-OCTA vessel-segmentation dataset, comprising 206 eyes from 152 participants with a 24x20mm^2 field of view. WOIVES spans emmetropia to high myopia and provides soft probability vessel annotations. We further propose PG-Mamba, a visual state space model that enhances conventional directional scans with two complementary polar-coordinate scan orders. An auxiliary Dynamic FOV Gating module performs spatial modulation at the bottleneck. PG-Mamba outperformed seven competitive approaches on broad segmentation metrics under cross-validation. It achieved the lowest median absolute errors for vessel density, fractal dimension, and vessel length density. WOIVES is publicly available on Zenodo (DOI: 10.5281/zenodo.21904672), and the PG-Mamba code is available at https://github.com/syb1234567/PG-Mamba.

</details>


### [35] [SCORE: SubDistribution-aware Collaborative Knowledge Reinforcing for Cloth-Hybrid Lifelong Person Re-Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12577)
*Kunlun Xu,Liangyu Ma,Jiangmeng Li,Xin Tong,Xiaode Liu,Yufei Guo,Jiahuan Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SCORE introduces adaptive subdistribution modeling and collaborative knowledge reinforcement to handle cloth-consistent and cloth-changing knowledge in CH-LReID, achieving state-of-the-art results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Lifelong Re-Identification (LReID) on non-stationary data streams faces catastrophic forgetting, compounded in Cloth-Hybrid LReID where cloth-changing data introduces conflicting cloth-relevant and cloth-irrelevant knowledge. Explicitly modeling intra-identity diversity is proposed to retain both knowledge types.

Method: Adaptive SubDistribution Modeling assigns multiple distributional subprototypes to each identity to capture intra-identity diversity (cloth-consistent and cloth-changing knowledge). Distributional Knowledge Reinforcement uses a collaborative aligning mechanism to retain knowledge of old subprototypes in new representations, mitigating forgetting across the data stream.

Result: Extensive experiments demonstrate that SCORE achieves state-of-the-art performance on CH-LReID benchmarks.

Conclusion: Modeling intra-identity diversity with subprototypes and enforcing cross-temporal alignment effectively mitigates catastrophic forgetting in CH-LReID lifelong learning, delivering superior retrieval performance; code is available at the authors’ repository.

Abstract: Lifelong Person Re-Identification (LReID) aims to train a unified person retrieval model from a non-stationary data stream. Existing LReID methods mainly focus on scenarios where the clothing of each person is consistent. Recently, the Cloth-Hybrid LReID (CH-LReID) where cloth-consistent and cloth-changing data alternately occur, has emerged as a more practical and challenging scenario. Due to the conflict between clothing-relevant and clothing-irrelevant knowledge, the well-known catastrophic forgetting problem is significantly exacerbated in this task. To address this issue, we propose a SubDistribution-aware COllaborative Knowledge REinforcing (SCORE) framework, where our key idea is explicitly modeling the intra-identity diversity to continually consolidate distinct cloth-consistent and cloth-changing knowledge. Specifically, an Adaptive SubDistribution Modeling mechanism is developed, where a set of distributional subprototypes is assigned to each identity to capture the intra-identity diversity, improving the compatibility between cloth-consistent and cloth-changing knowledge. Then, a Distributional Knowledge Reinforcement scheme is introduced, where the knowledge of old distributional subprototypes is retained in the new ones by a collaborative aligning mechanism. Extensive experiments show that our SCORE achieves the state-of-the-art performance.
  Our code is available at https://github.com/zhoujiahuan1991/ECCV2026-SCORE

</details>


### [36] [RA-SOD: Reliability-Aware RGB-T Salient Object Detection under Modality Degradation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12622)
*Hongbo Gao,Zhengyu Li,Xueru Nie,Dihao Zhu,Lijun Zhao,Yunke Wang,Chang Xu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RA-SOD introduces reliability-aware RGB-T saliency detection by modeling per-pixel modality reliability and fusing cues through a reliability-conditioned representation, an uncertainty-guided dual-stream refinement, and a pixel-wise modality competition; it achieves state-of-the-art robustness on four VT benchmarks with available code.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In RGB-T SOD, RGB and thermal modalities suffer from different degradation modes. Unreliable perceptual evidence can mislead cross-modal fusion and degrade performance; explicit reliability modeling is needed to maintain robust saliency under adverse conditions.

Method: Three components: (1) reliability-conditioned representation to adaptively compensate degraded modality features while preserving structure; (2) uncertainty-guided dual-stream refinement to progressively correct cross-modal representations and suppress unreliable evidence; (3) pixel-wise modality competition to dynamically select cues according to spatial reliability for fine-grained fusion.

Result: Extensive experiments on VT821, VT1000, VT5000, and VT-IMAG show state-of-the-art performance and strong robustness to severe modality degradation; code and models are released at the provided GitHub link.

Conclusion: Explicit reliability modeling in RGB-T SOD yields more robust cross-modal fusion and improved detection under challenging conditions; the proposed components offer a cohesive framework that can inspire similar reliability-aware multi-modal fusion methods.

Abstract: RGB-Thermal (RGB-T) salient object detection leverages complementary cues from visible and thermal modalities to improve robustness in challenging environments. However, in real-world scenarios, the reliability of each modality is inherently unstable: RGB images degrade under low illumination, motion blur, and noise, while thermal imagery often suffers from contrast compression and sensor artifacts. Such degradation introduces unreliable perceptual evidence that can mislead cross-modal fusion and significantly deteriorate detection performance. To address this challenge, we propose RA-SOD, a reliability-aware RGB-T salient object detection framework that explicitly models modality reliability and integrates it into feature learning and cross-modal fusion. First, we introduce a reliability-conditioned representation that adaptively compensates degraded modality features while preserving structural cues. Second, an uncertainty-guided dual-stream refinement strategy progressively corrects cross-modal representations while suppressing unreliable evidence. Finally, we propose a pixel-wise modality competition mechanism that dynamically selects modality cues according to spatial reliability for fine-grained fusion. Extensive experiments on four benchmarks (VT821, VT1000, VT5000, and VT-IMAG) demonstrate that RA-SOD achieves state-of-the-art performance and exhibits strong robustness under severe modality degradation. Code and models are available at https://github.com/zaoxienian/RA-SOD.

</details>


### [37] [Beyond Ambiguous Visual Cues: Studying Physiological Disruptions and Cross-Modal Inconsistencies in Deepfake Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12668)
*Chenxi Yang,Yassine Ouzar,Larbi Boubchir*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A bidirectional co-attention fusion detector jointly models rPPG signals and facial behavior for deepfake detection, addressing lack of physiological ground truth and cross-level feature interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing detectors often rely on single modalities or late fusion and lack physiological ground truth, limiting robustness to manipulations that alter both physiological signals and facial motion.

Method: Create high-fidelity deepfake manipulations on COHFACE and UBFC-rPPG; propose a bidirectional co-attention fusion detector that jointly models rPPG and facial behavior tokens, capturing cross-level dependencies between pulse dynamics and facial motion; subject-disjoint 5-fold evaluation.

Result: Achieves 92.80% AUC on face-swapping and 96.78% AUC on motion transfer; outperforms rPPG-only baselines and feature-level fusion methods; Celeb-DF-v2 transfer yields 91.20% accuracy and 86.08% AUC with fixed feature extractors.

Conclusion: Demonstrates the effectiveness of cross-level, joint representation learning for deepfake detection and shows potential for cross-domain adaptation.

Abstract: Recent deepfake detection studies increasingly suggest remote photoplethysmography (rPPG) signals as an authenticity cue. However, existing benchmarks lack physiological ground truth, and current detectors underexplore the cross-level relationship between facial features and physiological dynamics, often relying on late fusion or rPPG features alone. In this paper, we construct high-fidelity deepfake manipulations on established real rPPG datasets (COHFACE and UBFC-rPPG) to investigate how forgeries disrupt natural physiological signals and facial behavior at the same time. Building on this analysis, we propose a bidirectional co-attention fusion detector that jointly models rPPG and facial behavior tokens. This mechanism explicitly captures the cross-level dependencies between pulse dynamics and facial motion to learn a robust, joint authenticity representation. Extensive experiments using a subject-disjoint 5-fold evaluation demonstrate the superiority of our approach. Achieving a 92.80\% AUC on constructed datasets using face swapping and 96.78\% AUC on motion transfer, our model outperforms both the rPPG-only single modality baseline and the best feature-level fusion methods. Furthermore, transfer-learning result of the fusion detector on Celeb-DF-v2 while keeping both feature extractors fixed achieves 91.20\% accuracy and 86.08\% AUC, which suggests applicability under target-domain adaptation.

</details>


### [38] [Detecting and Explaining Fake News Short Videos with Multimodal Content and Real-World Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12678)
*Yifeng Luo,Yupeng Li,Ming Tang,Jianxiong Guo,Liang Lan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A unified NVKE-CEI pipeline for fake news video detection and explanation, combining keyframe extraction with multimodal evidence-based verification using LLMs and a lightweight judge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current fake-news video detectors struggle with selecting informative frames and integrating multimodal content with external evidence; this limits both accuracy and interpretability.

Method: Introduce NVKE-CEI: NVKE selects keyframes by tracking chronological changes in combined visual and OCR-text similarity; CEI uses two LLM-based fact-checkers (content-based and evidence-based) whose outputs are fused by a lightweight judge.

Result: Empirical results show state-of-the-art performance on FNVDE benchmarks and high-quality content-grounded explanations.

Conclusion: Demonstrates that a unified framework combining precise keyframe selection and evidence-aware explanation improves detection accuracy and interpretability; adaptable to other multimedia verification tasks.

Abstract: Short-video platforms have become a primary news source for the public, which has also enabled the widespread dissemination of fake news videos. We study the task of fake news video detection and explanation (FNVDE). Existing methods face two critical limitations. First, commonly used frame selection strategies may omit veracity-relevant cues or provide insufficient temporal context for understanding news videos. Second, prior methods neglect either multimodal understanding or evidence retrieval. To address these limitations, we propose NVKE-CEI, a unified system that integrates a news video keyframes extraction method (NVKE) and an FNVDE framework leveraging both content and evidence information (CEI). NVKE selects keyframes based on chronological changes in combined visual and OCR-text similarity. CEI employs two specialized LLM-based fact checkers (content-based and evidence-based) whose outputs are fused by a lightweight judge model. Extensive experiments show that NVKE-CEI outperforms state-of-the-art baselines while generating high-quality content-grounded explanations.

</details>


### [39] [NOVA-GS: Noise-Aware View-Consistent Gaussian Splatting for Low-Light Novel View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12682)
*Shaurya Pavan A,Vemunuri Divya Madhuri,Yash Pradeep Gawande,Kaushik Mitra*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Unified noise-aware 3D Gaussian Splatting for low-light scenes (NOVA-GS) that jointly enhances, denoises, and optimizes geometry with VGGT-based pose estimation, removing the need for SfM or well-lit references.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Low-light 3D reconstruction is hindered by severe sensor noise, low SNR, and photometric inconsistencies, which destabilize geometry and cause cross-view artifacts. Requiring well-lit references or per-view enhancement limits robustness and coherence.

Method: NOVA-GS uses VGGT-based feed-forward pose estimation on degraded inputs (no SfM). It then jointly optimizes: (1) structure-aware exposure correction, (2) self-supervised denoising with blind-spot masking for pseudo-supervision, and (3) consistency-driven Gaussian Splatting optimization enforcing cross-view geometric coherence. A noise-guided spherical harmonic regularization suppresses view-dependent artifacts in noisy regions.

Result: Extensive experiments on diverse real-world low-light datasets show improved geometric fidelity, color consistency, and robustness, without paired supervision or well-lit references.

Conclusion: A unified, noise-aware 3D reconstruction pipeline that combines enhancement, denoising, and geometry optimization in a single process can achieve robust 3D reconstruction under severe low-light conditions without SfM or external references.

Abstract: Reconstructing 3D scenes under real-world low-light conditions remains challenging due to severe sensor noise, low signal-to-noise ratios, and degraded photometric consistency, which destabilize geometry estimation and novel view synthesis. Existing approaches often rely on well-lit reference data for reliable Structure-from-Motion (SfM) initialization under degraded inputs or apply per-view enhancement methods that introduce cross-view inconsistencies. To address these limitations, we propose \textbf{NOVA-GS}, a unified noise-aware framework for low-light 3D Gaussian Splatting that subsumes enhancement, denoising, and geometry optimization within a single process. Our method leverages VGGT-based feed-forward estimation to obtain robust camera poses and geometry directly from degraded inputs, eliminating the need for SfM. Building on this initialization, NOVA-GS integrates three coupled components: a structure-aware enhancement module for exposure correction, a self-supervised denoising module with blind-spot masking for pseudo-supervision, and a consistency-driven Gaussian Splatting optimization enforcing cross-view geometric coherence. We further introduce a noise-guided spherical harmonic regularization to suppress view-dependent artifacts in noisy regions. Extensive experiments on diverse real-world low-light datasets demonstrate improved geometric fidelity, color consistency, and robustness without requiring paired supervision or well-lit references. https://shaurya2524.github.io/nova-gs/

</details>


### [40] [Semantically Aligned Gradient-Driven Context-Preserving Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12691)
*Chiranjeev Chiranjeev,Muskan Dosi,Mayank Vatsa,Richa Singh*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: IABEdit introduces training-time semantic verification via a frozen vision-language model and a trainable aligner to guide edits with gradient signals, yielding improved fidelity and localization across backbones without extra inference cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current instruction-guided editing lacks semantic verification during training; supervision ends at reconstruction and text conditioning, causing incomplete edits and poor localization, especially under occlusion.

Method: Freeze a vision-language model to extract spatial descriptors from ground-truth edits; train an aligner to reproduce these descriptors from the generated output; use their residual as gradients to teach the generator what and where to edit; compatible with backbones such as U-Net (Stable Diffusion) and MMDiT (FLUX) with no inference-time VLM cost.

Result: On MagicBrush, achieves +3.49 in DINO-I over the best diffusion baseline and +1.26 over the best overall baseline; attains state-of-the-art instruction adherence on RealEdit and EMU Edit; on the D-LORD surveillance benchmark under heavy occlusion, surpasses Gemini by +5.13 in DINO-P; human and GPT-4o evaluations confirm perceptually precise, well-localized edits.

Conclusion: Gradient-aligned VLM distillation provides effective training-time semantic verification, is model-agnostic with respect to backbone, does not alter inference pipelines, and yields robust, well-localized edits under challenging conditions.

Abstract: Instruction-guided image editing has a training-time blind spot. Generative editors are never required to semantically verify whether their outputs actually satisfy the instruction. Supervision stops at reconstruction and input textual-level conditioning. This produces incomplete edits, spatial spillover, and poor localization. We present IABEdit, a model-agnostic framework that embeds differentiable semantic verification into training. A frozen vision-language model extracts spatially-aware descriptors from the ground-truth edit. A trainable aligner then reproduces them from the generated output. The residual between the two becomes a gradient that teaches the generator both what to edit and where, with no inference-time VLM cost. IABEdit is compatible with diverse backbones, including U-Net (Stable Diffusion) and MMDiT (FLUX), without altering their inference pipelines. On MagicBrush, it improves structural fidelity by +3.49 DINO-I over the best diffusion baseline and +1.26 over the best overall baseline, while remaining competitive on instruction alignment. It also achieves state-of-the-art instruction adherence performance on RealEdit and EMU Edit benchmarks based on embedding-based metrics. Most consequentially, on the D-LORD surveillance benchmark, it surpasses the proprietary Gemini agent by +5.13 DINO-P under heavy occlusion, where preserving identity is hardest. This shows that gradient-aligned VLM distillation holds up under real-world-like surveillance and occlusion conditions. Human and GPT-4o evaluations confirm perceptually precise, well-localized edits.

</details>


### [41] [ExpertHTR: Unified Handwritten Text Recognition with Multi-Task Learning and Sparse Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12705)
*Dang Hoai Nam,Nguyen Duy Hieu,Quang Huu Hieu,Vo Nguyen Le Duy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A unified, multi-source handwriting text recognition model using complementary supervision and sparse routing to surpass general OCR baselines and achieve state-of-the-art on IAM paragraph-level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Handwritten datasets are heterogeneous in language/script/layout/annotations and resources are limited; joint page-level training across collections is difficult without additional labels.

Method: Organize structural annotations into a common Page-Region-Line representation to support four tasks (full transcription, line coverage, localizing text, and localized recognition). Train a dense shared model and then apply a sparse Mixture-of-Experts with an always-active shared branch and conditionally routed full-MLP experts. Use SparseGen to vary the number of active experts with the hidden state and routing regularization to avoid expert over-concentration.

Result: Across seven heterogeneous handwriting benchmarks, complementary supervision improves training with page transcription alone; multi-source training yields further gains on most datasets; the sparse expert model improves the dense baseline on six of seven sources; the unified model outperforms general-purpose OCR and vision-language systems on most benchmarks and achieves state-of-the-art on IAM paragraph-level, though specialized HTR systems remain stronger on some collections.

Conclusion: The combination of complementary supervision and conditional capacity enables effective multi-source handwriting recognition, enabling scalable training across heterogeneous datasets and yielding strong performance gains, including state-of-the-art on IAM paragraph-level.

Abstract: Handwritten text recognition resources are often small and distributed across collections that differ in language, script, document structure, and annotation format, making joint page-level training difficult. We propose ExpertHTR, a unified vision-language framework that addresses this problem through complementary supervision and conditional model capacity. Structural annotations from heterogeneous datasets are first organized through a common Page-Region-Line representation and used to construct four related training tasks for complete transcription, physical-line coverage, text localization, and localized recognition, without requiring additional manual labels. Building on a jointly trained dense model, ExpertHTR introduces a sparse Mixture-of-Experts architecture with an always-active shared branch and conditionally routed full-MLP experts. Sparsegen allows the number of active routed experts to vary with the hidden representation, while routing regularization reduces persistent concentration on a small subset of experts. Experiments on seven heterogeneous handwriting benchmarks show that complementary supervision consistently improves training with page transcription alone, while joint multi-source training provides further gains on most datasets. The proposed sparse expert model further improves the dense baseline on six of the seven sources. The final unified model also substantially outperforms the evaluated general-purpose OCR and vision-language systems on most benchmarks and achieves state-of-the-art performance on the IAM paragraph-level benchmark, while specialized HTR systems remain stronger on several challenging collections.

</details>


### [42] [Multimodal Floorplan Encoding: Learning Dense Modality-Invariant Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12723)
*Xavier Anadón,Rémi Pautrat,Rui Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MMFE introduces a multimodal floorplan encoder that maps diverse 2D representations (vector, raster, density maps) into a shared dense latent grid using a frozen DINOv3 backbone and a trainable Dense Prediction Transformer head, trained with a per-cell InfoNCE objective. It enforces geometric robustness via controlled similarity transforms and feature-grid warping, achieving improved cross-modal matching and retrieval on Structured3D (out-of-domain).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Floorplan data come in heterogeneous 2D representations across modalities, making cross-modal learning and geometry-centric tasks (alignment, retrieval) difficult. A common, geometry-aware latent representation can enable robust cross-modal analysis and transfer.

Method: Architectural: frozen DINOv3 backbone + trainable Dense Prediction Transformer (DPT) head to produce per-cell features mapped to a shared grid. Training objective: per-cell Information Noise-Contrastive Estimation (InfoNCE) that pulls spatially corresponding cells across modalities together while treating other cells as negatives. Robustness enhancements: apply controlled similarity transformations and enforce geometric consistency via feature-grid warping. Evaluation protocol: cross-modal dense matching and retrieval on Structured3D, a held-out out-of-domain dataset, with RANSAC for alignment and learned aggregation for retrieval.

Result: Empirical: MMFE improves cross-modal dense matching on Structured3D, enables robust similarity alignment with RANSAC, and achieves strong retrieval when paired with learned aggregation, demonstrating effective cross-modal transfer and geometry-aware representation.

Conclusion: MMFE provides a robust, geometry-conscious multimodal representation for floorplans by aligning diverse 2D representations into a shared latent grid and enforcing geometric consistency, enabling reliable cross-modal alignment and retrieval on out-of-domain data.

Abstract: Floorplans arise in many forms, from vector CAD drawings to raster renderings and sensor-derived density maps. This heterogeneity makes it difficult to build learning systems that transfer across modalities and support geometry-centric tasks such as alignment and retrieval. We introduce the Multimodal Floorplan Encoder (MMFE), which maps diverse 2D indoor representations into a shared dense latent grid. MMFE combines a frozen DINOv3 backbone with a trainable Dense Prediction Transformer (DPT) head, and is trained with a per-cell Information Noise-Contrastive Estimation (InfoNCE) objective that aligns spatially corresponding regions across modalities while using all other cells as negatives. To improve robustness to geometric distortions, we incorporate controlled similarity transformations and enforce geometric consistency through feature-grid warping. On Structured3D, a held-out out-of-domain dataset, MMFE improves cross-modal dense matching, enables robust similarity alignment with RANSAC, and yields strong retrieval when paired with learned aggregation.

</details>


### [43] [GRACE: Adaptive Concept Erasure with Geometry-Guided Retention in Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12731)
*Qinghui Gong,Yihuai Liang,Yuanlun Xie,Deepak Kumar Jain,Vitomir Štruc,Zhengchun Zhou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GRACE is a structured concept erasure framework for text-to-image diffusion models that combines semantically weighted subspace estimation, subspace-constrained adapters, an automatic safe-anchor mechanism, and energy-driven dynamic gating to achieve localized erasure with minimal semantic drift and preserved generative quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome over-intervention and semantic drift in post-hoc concept erasure, avoid manual surrogate prompts, and enable scalable erasure across new concepts with minimal data construction cost.

Method: (1) Semantically weighted sensitive subspace estimation to lock intervention directions; (2) Lightweight subspace-constrained adapters to prevent global semantic disturbance; (3) Automatically decoupled safe-anchor mechanism to remove dependence on manual prompts; (4) Energy-driven dynamic gating to adapt timing and strength of intervention at inference to mitigate semantic drift.

Result: Outperforms five SOTA concept erasure methods by: fine-grained NSFW reduction improved by 17.86%; macro-averaged target CLIP Score reduced by 4.75%; preservation-oriented FID reduced by 50.58%, indicating stronger concept suppression with substantially preserved generative utility.

Conclusion: GRACE achieves a favorable trade-off between erasure effectiveness and generation fidelity, enabling precise, localized parameter updates and scalable, automatic supervision for robust concept erasure in T2I diffusion models.

Abstract: Text-to-image (T2I) diffusion models inevitably internalize sensitive or non-compliant concepts from large-scale pretraining data, necessitating post-hoc concept erasure. However, existing erasure methods often lack explicit constraints on parameter updates, leading to over-intervention and unintended semantic drift. In addition, many methods rely on manually crafted counterfactual supervision, such as surrogate prompts, which incurs substantial data construction costs that limit scalability to new concepts. To address these limitations, we propose GRACE, a structured concept erasure framework designed to enable localized and selective intervention. Specifically, we introduce a semantically weighted sensitive subspace estimation to precisely lock intervention directions, and employ lightweight subspace-constrained adapters to prevent global semantic disturbance. To eliminate the dependency on manual prompt engineering, we design an automatically decoupled safe-anchor mechanism. To mitigate semantic drift induced by excessive intervention, we introduce an energy-driven dynamic gating mechanism that adaptively controls the timing and strength of intervention at inference. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves a superior balance between erasure effectiveness and generation fidelity. Compared with the average performance of five state-of-the-art (SOTA) concept erasure methods, our method improves the fine-grained NSFW reduction rate by $17.86\%$, while reducing the macro-averaged target CLIP Score and preservation-oriented Fréchet Inception Distance (FID) by $4.75\%$ and $50.58\%$, respectively, indicating stronger concept suppression with substantially improved preservation of the original model's generative utility.

</details>


### [44] [AquaCubeAI-Powered Monitoring Turbidity on-board Φsat-2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12744)
*Pietro Di Stasio,Francesca Razzano,Elisa Liparulo,Gabriele Meoni,Nicolas Longépé,Deodato Tapete,Paolo Gamba,Gilda Schirinzi,Silvia Liberata Ullo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AquaCubeAI uses a compact MLP to estimate coastal water turbidity onboard Φsat-2 imagery, trained on simulated Φsat-2 patches with CMEMS HR-OC turbidity labels, evaluated with a leakage-aware geospatial split, and deployed on an Intel Myriad VPU for low-latency, dense turbidity mapping and anomaly masks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reduce latency in coastal turbidity monitoring by moving inference from ground to spaceborne hardware, enabling faster, more operational responses under tight compute and bandwidth constraints.

Method: Create a scalable dataset by aligning simulated Φsat-2 multispectral patches with CMEMS HR-OC turbidity labels across European coastal sites; train a compact MLP regressor under leakage-aware geospatial splitting; use parameter sharing for dense spatial predictions; generate threshold-based anomaly masks for onboard decision logic; deploy on Intel Myriad VPU for low-power inference on multispectral inputs.

Result: Feasibility demonstrated for onboard, low-power turbidity estimation with AquaCubeAI, showing potential for low-latency turbidity mapping and anomaly detection under spaceborne constraints; evaluation uses leakage-aware spatial splits to support honest generalization.

Conclusion: Onboard turbidity monitoring using a lightweight ML approach is feasible and beneficial, though the study relies on simulated data aligned to CMEMS labels; further work should address real flight data, broader geographic generalization, and quantitative performance metrics.

Abstract: Timely monitoring of coastal water quality is critical for environmental protection, yet conventional satellite workflows rely on downlink and ground processing, introducing latency that can limit responsiveness to rapidly evolving turbidity events. To address this limitation, we propose AquaCubeAI, a lightweight machine-learning approach for onboard estimation of coastal water turbidity from Φsat-2 multispectral imagery. By shifting inference from the ground segment to the satellite, AquaCubeAI aims to enable lower-latency, more responsive, and more operationally useful turbidity monitoring under the strict compute and bandwidth constraints of spaceborne platforms. The model is trained on simulated Φsat-2 acquisitions spatially aligned with Copernicus Marine Service (CMEMS) High-Resolution Ocean Color (HR-OC) turbidity products over selected localized coastal sites spanning four European marine macro-regions. To provide a realistic evaluation of generalization in the presence of spatial correlation, we adopt a spatial block splitting protocol that mitigates data leakage between training and evaluation subsets. The main contributions of this work are: (i) a scalable dataset generation pipeline pairing simulated Φsat-2 multispectral patches with CMEMS HR-OC turbidity labels across selected localized European coastal sites; (ii) a compact Multi-Layer Perceptron (MLP)-based turbidity regressor trained under a leakage-aware geospatial split and tailored to embedded constraints; and (iii) a reformulation for dense spatial prediction via parameter sharing, enabling turbidity mapping and simple threshold-based anomaly masks for onboard decision logic. Embedded deployment on an Intel Myriad Vision Processing Unit (VPU) further confirms the feasibility of low-power hardware and supports low-latency inference from multispectral inputs.

</details>


### [45] [Same Encoder, Different Winner: A Paired-View Framework for Cell Painting Encoder Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12761)
*Tim Treis,Nikita Moshkov,Johan Fredin Haslum,Shantanu Singh,Fabian J. Theis*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A four-view, paired-view evaluation (CP-BG-Bench) reveals that single-metric encoder rankings for Cell Painting are highly protocol-dependent, with disagreements decomposing into cell/background, morphology/context, and within-study vs cross-batch axes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Relying on a single evaluation metric (e.g., replicate mAP) obscures how encoders leverage cell versus background, morphology vs context, and batch-related signals. A controlled, multi-view framework across datasets and encoders is needed to understand generalization and interpretability of vision encoders for Cell Painting.

Method: Introduce CP-BG-Bench with four paired views: raw crop (C), segmented (S), and density-augmented variants (CD, SD). Fix the central cell across views, ablate or augment surrounding pixels, and evaluate on three datasets (JUMP-CP, RxRx1, RxRx3-core) and three encoders (DINOv3 ViT-B/16, OpenPhenom, SubCell) under four protocols: replicate mAP, scIB batch integration, CellProfiler feature prediction, and cross-batch perturbation recall.

Result: Across protocols, encoder rankings diverge systematically. Disagreements decompose along three axes: cell vs background, morphology vs context, and within-study vs across-batch. A notable example: SubCell on RxRx3-core retains 94% of crop replicate mAP but only 32% of crop R@10. Density augmentation recovers 84% of the within-study C-to-S gap but only 8% of the cross-batch gap. Segmented views predict CellProfiler features as well as or better than crops on two of three datasets, inverting replicate-mAP rankings. The C-to-S gap varies by dataset but is similar across encoders, indicating background gain is design-dependent. Overall, single-metric ranking is protocol-sensitive; disagreements correspond to interpretable axes exposed by the paired-view design. The authors plan to release datasets, pipelines, 36 checkpoints, aggregated embeddings, and the full evaluation suite.”

Conclusion: Paired-view evaluation clarifies how protocol choices shape encoder rankings and what signals (cell, background, morphology, batch) drive performance. Multi-metric, multi-view evaluation is essential for robust interpretation and generalization in Cell Painting encoders; the authors provide resources to support this community effort.

Abstract: Vision encoders for Cell Painting are typically ranked by a single evaluation, commonly replicate mean average precision (mAP). We introduce CP-BG-Bench, a paired-view evaluation framework that holds the central cell fixed across four matched views (raw crop C, segmented S, and density-augmented variants CD and SD), ablating or augmenting surrounding pixels as a controlled intervention. Instantiating the framework on three datasets (JUMP-CP, RxRx1, RxRx3-core) and three encoders (DINOv3 ViT-B/16, OpenPhenom, SubCell) under four community-standard protocols (replicate mAP, scIB batch integration, CellProfiler feature prediction, cross-batch perturbation recall), we find that the four protocols rank the same encoders systematically differently, with disagreements decomposing along three axes: cell versus background, morphology versus context, and within-study versus across-batch. The largest effect: on RxRx3-core, SubCell with segmented inputs retains 94% of crop replicate mAP but only 32% of crop R@10, so the within-study signal preserved under segmentation is largely non-transferable; density augmentation recovers 84% of the within-study C-to-S gap but only 8% of the cross-batch gap. Segmented views predict CellProfiler features as well as or better than crops on two of three datasets, inverting the replicate-mAP ranking, and the C-to-S gap varies by an order of magnitude across datasets while remaining similar across encoders, indicating that background-driven gain is set by experimental design rather than by the encoder. Single-metric ranking of Cell Painting encoders is therefore sensitive to the protocol used, and protocol disagreements are interpretable as projections onto the three axes the paired-view design exposes. We will release the paired-view datasets, reconstruction pipelines, 36 trained checkpoints, aggregated embeddings, and the full evaluation suite.

</details>


### [46] [LG-PF: Lightweight Confidence-Guided Polarization Image Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12787)
*Zhuangfan Huang,Zhenyu Kuang,Gao Wang,Yang Liu,Haishu Tan,Xiaosong Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A lightweight, confidence-guided polarization fusion framework (LG-PF) that selectively transfers polarization details to a total-intensity image using a polarization confidence prior, a Mask-guided Multi-scale Fusion module, and a context-aware correction head; accompanied by MSP, a multi-scene polarization fusion dataset. Achieves state-of-the-art fusion on MSP with 0.2936M parameters and fast inference, and shows transferability to PIF and GAND without fine-tuning; source code and data to be released.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: DoLP reliability varies spatially, and indiscriminate polarization transfer can degrade structural fidelity anchored by the total-intensity image S0. A confidence-guided selective transfer aims to preserve reliable polarized details while suppressing unreliable responses.

Method: Introduce Polarization Confidence Prior to estimate spatially reliable polarization responses; implement Mask-guided Multi-scale Fusion across three feature scales to regulate transfer; propose Lightweight Context-aware Bounded Correction Head to stabilize local photometric/structural transitions; incorporate confidence guidance into optimization objectives; construct MSP dataset with 1000 pixel-aligned pairs from 17 indoor/outdoor scenes; achieve 0.2936M parameters and 21.712 ms per image.

Result: LG-PF achieves the best results across all six evaluated metrics on MSP; subset evaluations on PIF and GAND show promising transferability without fine-tuning; demonstrates competitive fusion quality with low computational cost.

Conclusion: LG-PF provides effective, efficient polarization fusion with confidence-guided selective transfer and introduces MSP dataset; code, dataset, and data splits will be publicly released upon publication.

Abstract: Polarization image fusion combines the stable luminance and structural information of the total- intensity image S0 with the material-sensitive details of the degree of linear polarization (DoLP) image. However, the reliability of DoLP varies spatially, and indiscriminate polarization transfer may amplify unstable responses or disturb the structural appearance anchored by S0. We therefore propose LG-PF, a lightweight confidence-guided framework that formulates polarization fusion as a selective residual transfer process. A Polarization Confidence Prior estimates spatially reliable polarization responses, a Mask-guided Multi-scale Fusion module regulates their transfer across three feature scales, and a Lightweight Context-aware Bounded Correction Head stabilizes local photometric and structural transitions. Confidence guidance is also incorporated into the optimization objectives to preserve reliable polarization details while suppressing unsupported responses. We also construct MSP, a multi-scene polarization fusion dataset containing 1000 pixel-aligned image pairs from 17 indoor and outdoor scene categories. LG-PF achieves the best results across all six evaluated metrics on MSP, while subset-based evaluations on PIF and GAND show promising transferability without fine-tuning. With only 0.2936 M parameters and an inference time of 21.712 ms per image, LG-PF achieves competitive fusion quality with low computational cost. The source code, dataset, and official data splits will be made publicly available upon publication.

</details>


### [47] [LGFN: Lightweight Gated RGB-Polarization Fusion with Modality-Availability Conditioning for Camouflaged Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12798)
*Zhuangfan Huang,Xiaosong Li,Yang Liu,Tao Ye,Haishu Tan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A lightweight RGB-polarization fusion framework (LGFN) for camouflaged object detection that supports RGB-only and polarization-assisted configurations with a modality router, achieving state-of-the-art RGB-based metrics and notable efficiency gains over IPNet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: COD is hard when targets blend with the background. Polarization imaging provides complementary cues, but existing methods rely on fixed input configurations and mix RGB with polarization in an entangled way.

Method: LGFN introduces a modular fusion system with a deterministic Modality Router to choose between RGB-only and multimodal modes; an availability-conditioned Modality Gate to adapt polarization branches; a Gated Polarization Hub to fuse DoLP and AoP cues; and RGB-Polarization Cross Fusion to inject calibrated polarization-aware representations into the RGB hierarchy via controlled residual connections. The design avoids sample-dependent statistics or handcrafted quality descriptors at inference and supports efficient operation.

Result: On PCOD_1200 (230-image test), RGB-only: MAE 0.0090, Dice 0.8806, IoU 0.8144; best among RGB-based methods on six metrics. Under local reevaluation, the multimodal configuration surpasses PolarNet and IPNet on all six metrics. Relative to IPNet, LGFN reduces parameters by 53.1%, FLOPs by 73.6%, and latency by 63.0%.

Conclusion: LGFN offers a flexible, efficient COD solution that performs strongly in RGB-only mode and gains robustness and efficiency in multimodal mode without relying on sample-specific inference statistics.

Abstract: Camouflaged object detection (COD) is an important engineering task in intelligent optical perception, but it remains challenging when targets closely resemble their surroundings. Polarization imaging provides complementary physical cues, whereas existing methods typically assume fixed multimodal input configurations and entangle intra-polarization coordination with interaction between red-green-blue (RGB) and polarization representations. We propose LGFN, a lightweight gated RGB-polarization fusion framework supporting separately optimized RGB-only and polarization-assisted configurations. A deterministic Modality Router selects the appropriate configuration according to polarization availability. In the multimodal configuration, an availability-conditioned Modality Gate calibrates the available polarization branches; the Gated Polarization Hub coordinates learned degree of linear polarization (DoLP) and angle of polarization (AoP) representations with explicit polarization cues; and RGB-Polarization Cross Fusion introduces the coordinated representation into the RGB hierarchy through controlled residual interaction. The multimodal configuration requires neither sample-dependent statistics nor handcrafted quality descriptors during inference. On the complete 230-image PCOD_1200 test set, the RGB-only configuration achieves a mean absolute error of 0.0090, a Dice score of 0.8806, and an intersection over union of 0.8144, obtaining the best results on all six metrics among the evaluated RGB-based methods. Under a common local reevaluation protocol, the multimodal configuration outperforms PolarNet and IPNet on all six metrics. Relative to IPNet, it reduces the parameter count, floating-point operations, and latency by 53.1%, 73.6%, and 63.0%, respectively.

</details>


### [48] [Online Video Agent Harness for Long Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12818)
*Sen Yang,Boqiang Duan,Jing Yang,Weihao Bo,Jie Liu,Boyuan Tong,Ze Feng,Wenkang Zhang,Jingdong Wang,Hua Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VideoXAgent is an online long-video understanding framework that plans tasks, invokes on-demand heterogeneous tools (detection, OCR, ASR, face recognition, etc.), and aggregates evidence to answer queries with a light context footprint, matching frontier models on long videos while using far less context.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long videos pose a sparse-evidence problem (needle-in-a-haystack) and full-frame dense context incurs context rot and high costs. Existing approaches rely on offline preprocessing or broad toolsets that miss query-specific signals and waste computation.
